> help
One action = one game-hour; queries take no time. Sleep locks 4 hours.
Eat defaults to ONE serving; use eat hardtack 2 for two. Need effects use the previous hour's activity.

1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern.
1855-04-01 00:00 It has been a day. The ration is at 50 in the hundred and the children no longer ask. That is the worst of it: they no longer ask.

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern.
1855-04-01 00:00 It has been a day. The ration is at 50 in the hundred and the children no longer ask. That is the worst of it: they no longer ask.
1855-04-01 07:00 Honora Sheehan helped Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Honora Sheehan for it.
1855-04-01 08:00 Timothy Costello settled with Patrick Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Sunday in the forenoon. One more account closed in a county full of open ones.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. Full moon, and bright enough to read a letter by.
1855-04-02 06:00 Honora Sheehan came by again with something for the pot, and would not stay to be thanked. I am learning to let a gift be a gift.
1855-04-02 08:00 Monday. Timothy Costello comforted me, unasked. There are people in this county who keep the word neighbor alive.
1855-04-02 09:00 I made eyes at Timothy Costello. Whether it was welcome I cannot tell; the face he made could be read either way.
1855-04-02 11:00 It was the sensible thing; anyone would agree. I have asked myself and I agree.
1855-04-02 12:00 I was in close talk with Submit Crane at Crane General Store and Outfitter on Monday at noon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 14:00 I was in close talk with Silas Crane at Crane General Store and Outfitter on Monday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 15:00 Patrick Sheehan borrowed of Honora Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern. Money passes, and so do obligations; I have begun to keep a list.
1855-04-02 18:00 I drank a toast with Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening.
1855-04-02 18:00 Patrick Sheehan helped Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Patrick Sheehan for it.
1855-04-02 19:00 I had every reason for it, and I have written the reasons down so I shall not lose them. They are good ones. They read better each time.
1855-04-02 19:00 Miss Ellis Wayfarer was seen in earnest talk with Mr. T. Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening; there was a look of news about it.
1855-04-02 19:00 I was in close talk with Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 19:00 Timothy Costello was in close talk with me at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 20:00 Monday. Timothy Costello slighted me at The Wagon Bell Tavern, and I shall not soon forget it.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.
1855-04-03 06:00 Honora Sheehan came by again with something for the pot, and would not stay to be thanked. I am learning to let a gift be a gift.
1855-04-03 06:00 It is agreeable to relate that Miss Ellis Wayfarer was seen carrying relief to the door of Mrs. H. Sheehan, whose table has been poor of late. These things are noticed, and recorded here for the credit of the place.
1855-04-03 06:00 I have been doing what I can for Honora Sheehan. It comes out of my own stores. I do not mind as much as I feared.

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern.
1855-04-01 00:00 It has been a day. The ration is at 50 in the hundred and the children no longer ask. That is the worst of it: they no longer ask.
1855-04-01 07:00 Honora Sheehan helped Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Honora Sheehan for it.
1855-04-01 08:00 Timothy Costello settled with Patrick Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Sunday in the forenoon. One more account closed in a county full of open ones.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. Full moon, and bright enough to read a letter by.
1855-04-02 06:00 Honora Sheehan came by again with something for the pot, and would not stay to be thanked. I am learning to let a gift be a gift.
1855-04-02 08:00 Monday. Timothy Costello comforted me, unasked. There are people in this county who keep the word neighbor alive.
1855-04-02 09:00 I made eyes at Timothy Costello. Whether it was welcome I cannot tell; the face he made could be read either way.
1855-04-02 11:00 It was the sensible thing; anyone would agree. I have asked myself and I agree.
1855-04-02 12:00 I was in close talk with Submit Crane at Crane General Store and Outfitter on Monday at noon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 14:00 I was in close talk with Silas Crane at Crane General Store and Outfitter on Monday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 15:00 Patrick Sheehan borrowed of Honora Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern. Money passes, and so do obligations; I have begun to keep a list.
1855-04-02 18:00 I drank a toast with Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening.
1855-04-02 18:00 Patrick Sheehan helped Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Patrick Sheehan for it.
1855-04-02 19:00 I had every reason for it, and I have written the reasons down so I shall not lose them. They are good ones. They read better each time.
1855-04-02 19:00 Miss Ellis Wayfarer was seen in earnest talk with Mr. T. Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening; there was a look of news about it.
1855-04-02 19:00 I was in close talk with Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 19:00 Timothy Costello was in close talk with me at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 20:00 Monday. Timothy Costello slighted me at The Wagon Bell Tavern, and I shall not soon forget it.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.
1855-04-03 06:00 Honora Sheehan came by again with something for the pot, and would not stay to be thanked. I am learning to let a gift be a gift.
1855-04-03 06:00 It is agreeable to relate that Miss Ellis Wayfarer was seen carrying relief to the door of Mrs. H. Sheehan, whose table has been poor of late. These things are noticed, and recorded here for the credit of the place.
1855-04-03 06:00 I have been doing what I can for Honora Sheehan. It comes out of my own stores. I do not mind as much as I feared.
1855-04-03 10:00 Wilhelmine Strauss helped Johann Strauss at Strauss Ford Smithy. I saw it, and thought the better of Wilhelmine Strauss for it.
1855-04-03 14:00 Marit Vik comforted me at Vik Parsonage on Tuesday in the afternoon, and would not hear thanks. Entered here so I do not forget the debt.
1855-04-03 15:00 I confessed to Marit Vik at Vik Parsonage on Tuesday in the afternoon.
1855-04-03 18:00 Patrick Sheehan helped Johanna Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Patrick Sheehan for it.
1855-04-03 19:00 I lent money to Honora Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern. Money passes, and so do obligations; I have begun to keep a list.
1855-04-03 19:00 Honora Sheehan settled with me at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Tuesday of an evening. One more account closed in a county full of open ones.
1855-04-04 00:00 Wednesday, the 4th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous.
1855-04-04 06:00 Honora Sheehan helped Johanna Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Honora Sheehan for it.
1855-04-04 07:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-04 07:00 I insulted Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Wednesday in the forenoon, and was sorry before the day was out.
1855-04-04 07:00 Honora Sheehan comforted Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Honora Sheehan for it.

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern.
1855-04-01 00:00 It has been a day. The ration is at 50 in the hundred and the children no longer ask. That is the worst of it: they no longer ask.
1855-04-01 07:00 Honora Sheehan helped Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Honora Sheehan for it.
1855-04-01 08:00 Timothy Costello settled with Patrick Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Sunday in the forenoon. One more account closed in a county full of open ones.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. Full moon, and bright enough to read a letter by.
1855-04-02 06:00 Honora Sheehan came by again with something for the pot, and would not stay to be thanked. I am learning to let a gift be a gift.
1855-04-02 08:00 Monday. Timothy Costello comforted me, unasked. There are people in this county who keep the word neighbor alive.
1855-04-02 09:00 I made eyes at Timothy Costello. Whether it was welcome I cannot tell; the face he made could be read either way.
1855-04-02 11:00 It was the sensible thing; anyone would agree. I have asked myself and I agree.
1855-04-02 12:00 I was in close talk with Submit Crane at Crane General Store and Outfitter on Monday at noon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 14:00 I was in close talk with Silas Crane at Crane General Store and Outfitter on Monday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 15:00 Patrick Sheehan borrowed of Honora Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern. Money passes, and so do obligations; I have begun to keep a list.
1855-04-02 18:00 I drank a toast with Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening.
1855-04-02 18:00 Patrick Sheehan helped Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Patrick Sheehan for it.
1855-04-02 19:00 I had every reason for it, and I have written the reasons down so I shall not lose them. They are good ones. They read better each time.
1855-04-02 19:00 Miss Ellis Wayfarer was seen in earnest talk with Mr. T. Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening; there was a look of news about it.
1855-04-02 19:00 I was in close talk with Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 19:00 Timothy Costello was in close talk with me at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 20:00 Monday. Timothy Costello slighted me at The Wagon Bell Tavern, and I shall not soon forget it.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.
1855-04-03 06:00 Honora Sheehan came by again with something for the pot, and would not stay to be thanked. I am learning to let a gift be a gift.
1855-04-03 06:00 It is agreeable to relate that Miss Ellis Wayfarer was seen carrying relief to the door of Mrs. H. Sheehan, whose table has been poor of late. These things are noticed, and recorded here for the credit of the place.
1855-04-03 06:00 I have been doing what I can for Honora Sheehan. It comes out of my own stores. I do not mind as much as I feared.
1855-04-03 10:00 Wilhelmine Strauss helped Johann Strauss at Strauss Ford Smithy. I saw it, and thought the better of Wilhelmine Strauss for it.
1855-04-03 14:00 Marit Vik comforted me at Vik Parsonage on Tuesday in the afternoon, and would not hear thanks. Entered here so I do not forget the debt.
1855-04-03 15:00 I confessed to Marit Vik at Vik Parsonage on Tuesday in the afternoon.
1855-04-03 18:00 Patrick Sheehan helped Johanna Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Patrick Sheehan for it.
1855-04-03 19:00 I lent money to Honora Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern. Money passes, and so do obligations; I have begun to keep a list.
1855-04-03 19:00 Honora Sheehan settled with me at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Tuesday of an evening. One more account closed in a county full of open ones.
1855-04-04 00:00 Wednesday, the 4th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous.
1855-04-04 06:00 Honora Sheehan helped Johanna Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Honora Sheehan for it.
1855-04-04 07:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-04 07:00 I insulted Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Wednesday in the forenoon, and was sorry before the day was out.
1855-04-04 07:00 Honora Sheehan comforted Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Honora Sheehan for it.
1855-04-04 08:00 More trouble between the Sheehans and the Newcomer lodgingses: two wrongs now in a day, by my count. I keep out of it, which in Iron Ford is a labor.
1855-04-04 13:00 Patrick Sheehan helped Honora Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Patrick Sheehan for it.
1855-04-04 14:00 Miss Ellis Wayfarer was seen in earnest talk with Mrs. H. Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Wednesday in the afternoon; there was a look of news about it.
1855-04-04 14:00 I was in close talk with Honora Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Wednesday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-04 16:00 Patrick Sheehan came by again with something for the pot, and would not stay to be thanked. I am learning to let a gift be a gift.
1855-04-05 00:00 Thursday, the 5th of April, 1855, under the waning gibbous.
1855-04-05 07:00 Timothy Costello forgave me at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I had meant to hold out longer; I did not have the heart to.
1855-04-05 17:00 Patrick Sheehan came by again with something for the pot, and would not stay to be thanked. I am learning to let a gift be a gift.
1855-04-06 00:00 Friday, the 6th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous.

1855-04-06 10:00 | FAIR | The Wagon Bell Tavern
Ellis Wayfarer — at home, at leisure. Purse $44.15. 44°F, dry.
Food 5.9 days; Medium, 32.9 kg.
Vitals: Drink 865; Hunger 786; Rest 1000; Thirst 888; Warmth 1000
Counter open; hours 6:00–23:00.
Board: bed $0.25 until noon; meal $0.20 (type meal).
Here: Patrick Sheehan (#3), Honora Sheehan (#4), Johanna Sheehan (#5), Timothy Costello (#29).
Appetite: I keep going over what Timothy Costello did.
Conscience: Is Insult any way to treat a neighbor?
Self-Command: Resolved: Court
Heard: Timothy Costello is of the hidden folk

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern.
1855-04-01 00:00 It has been a day. The ration is at 50 in the hundred and the children no longer ask. That is the worst of it: they no longer ask.
1855-04-01 07:00 Honora Sheehan helped Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Honora Sheehan for it.
1855-04-01 08:00 Timothy Costello settled with Patrick Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Sunday in the forenoon. One more account closed in a county full of open ones.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. Full moon, and bright enough to read a letter by.
1855-04-02 06:00 Honora Sheehan came by again with something for the pot, and would not stay to be thanked. I am learning to let a gift be a gift.
1855-04-02 08:00 Monday. Timothy Costello comforted me, unasked. There are people in this county who keep the word neighbor alive.
1855-04-02 09:00 I made eyes at Timothy Costello. Whether it was welcome I cannot tell; the face he made could be read either way.
1855-04-02 11:00 It was the sensible thing; anyone would agree. I have asked myself and I agree.
1855-04-02 12:00 I was in close talk with Submit Crane at Crane General Store and Outfitter on Monday at noon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 14:00 I was in close talk with Silas Crane at Crane General Store and Outfitter on Monday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 15:00 Patrick Sheehan borrowed of Honora Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern. Money passes, and so do obligations; I have begun to keep a list.
1855-04-02 18:00 I drank a toast with Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening.
1855-04-02 18:00 Patrick Sheehan helped Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Patrick Sheehan for it.
1855-04-02 19:00 I had every reason for it, and I have written the reasons down so I shall not lose them. They are good ones. They read better each time.
1855-04-02 19:00 Miss Ellis Wayfarer was seen in earnest talk with Mr. T. Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening; there was a look of news about it.
1855-04-02 19:00 I was in close talk with Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 19:00 Timothy Costello was in close talk with me at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Monday of an evening. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 20:00 Monday. Timothy Costello slighted me at The Wagon Bell Tavern, and I shall not soon forget it.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.
1855-04-03 06:00 Honora Sheehan came by again with something for the pot, and would not stay to be thanked. I am learning to let a gift be a gift.
1855-04-03 06:00 It is agreeable to relate that Miss Ellis Wayfarer was seen carrying relief to the door of Mrs. H. Sheehan, whose table has been poor of late. These things are noticed, and recorded here for the credit of the place.
1855-04-03 06:00 I have been doing what I can for Honora Sheehan. It comes out of my own stores. I do not mind as much as I feared.
1855-04-03 10:00 Wilhelmine Strauss helped Johann Strauss at Strauss Ford Smithy. I saw it, and thought the better of Wilhelmine Strauss for it.
1855-04-03 14:00 Marit Vik comforted me at Vik Parsonage on Tuesday in the afternoon, and would not hear thanks. Entered here so I do not forget the debt.
1855-04-03 15:00 I confessed to Marit Vik at Vik Parsonage on Tuesday in the afternoon.
1855-04-03 18:00 Patrick Sheehan helped Johanna Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Patrick Sheehan for it.
1855-04-03 19:00 I lent money to Honora Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern. Money passes, and so do obligations; I have begun to keep a list.
1855-04-03 19:00 Honora Sheehan settled with me at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Tuesday of an evening. One more account closed in a county full of open ones.
1855-04-04 00:00 Wednesday, the 4th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous.
1855-04-04 06:00 Honora Sheehan helped Johanna Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Honora Sheehan for it.
1855-04-04 07:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-04 07:00 I insulted Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Wednesday in the forenoon, and was sorry before the day was out.
1855-04-04 07:00 Honora Sheehan comforted Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Honora Sheehan for it.
1855-04-04 08:00 More trouble between the Sheehans and the Newcomer lodgingses: two wrongs now in a day, by my count. I keep out of it, which in Iron Ford is a labor.
1855-04-04 13:00 Patrick Sheehan helped Honora Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of Patrick Sheehan for it.
1855-04-04 14:00 Miss Ellis Wayfarer was seen in earnest talk with Mrs. H. Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Wednesday in the afternoon; there was a look of news about it.
1855-04-04 14:00 I was in close talk with Honora Sheehan at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Wednesday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-04 16:00 Patrick Sheehan came by again with something for the pot, and would not stay to be thanked. I am learning to let a gift be a gift.
1855-04-05 00:00 Thursday, the 5th of April, 1855, under the waning gibbous.
1855-04-05 07:00 Timothy Costello forgave me at The Wagon Bell Tavern. I had meant to hold out longer; I did not have the heart to.
1855-04-05 17:00 Patrick Sheehan came by again with something for the pot, and would not stay to be thanked. I am learning to let a gift be a gift.
1855-04-06 00:00 Friday, the 6th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous.
1855-04-06 09:00 I paid court to Timothy Costello at The Wagon Bell Tavern on Friday in the forenoon. My heart did a thing it has no business doing in a person of sense.
